# Supabase Columns

## Purpose

Capture the current column-level structure of tables and views in the CAFLA Supabase database.

This inventory documents the actual database structure found at the time of the audit, including column names, PostgreSQL data types, nullability, defaults, and relevant type metadata.

The presence or structure of an object does not determine whether that object is currently active or used by the CAFLA application.

---

## Scope

Schemas included:

- `public`
- `tournaments`
- `development`

Objects included:

- Base tables
- Standard views

Column metadata captured:

- Schema
- Table/view name
- Column position
- Column name
- SQL data type
- PostgreSQL underlying type (`udt_name`)
- Nullability
- Default value
- Character maximum length
- Numeric precision
- Numeric scale

---

## Query

```sql
select
  table_schema,
  table_name,
  ordinal_position,
  column_name,
  data_type,
  udt_name,
  is_nullable,
  column_default,
  character_maximum_length,
  numeric_precision,
  numeric_scale
from information_schema.columns
where table_schema in ('public', 'tournaments', 'development')
order by
  table_schema,
  table_name,
  ordinal_position;
```

---

## Result Interpretation

Each result represents one column belonging to a table or view.

Important fields:

- `table_schema` — PostgreSQL schema containing the object.
- `table_name` — table or view containing the column.
- `ordinal_position` — physical/logical position of the column.
- `column_name` — column name.
- `data_type` — SQL-level data type reported by `information_schema`.
- `udt_name` — underlying PostgreSQL type. This is especially useful for UUIDs, enums, arrays, and user-defined types.
- `is_nullable` — whether the column accepts `NULL`.
- `column_default` — database-level default expression, if one exists.
- `character_maximum_length` — maximum character length where applicable.
- `numeric_precision` and `numeric_scale` — numeric constraints where applicable.

A `null` value for optional metadata does not imply an error. It generally means that property does not apply to that column.

---

## Audit Notes

This file captures database structure only.

It does not establish:

- Whether a table/view is actively used.
- Whether a column is referenced by application code.
- Whether a column is obsolete.
- Primary key membership.
- Foreign key relationships.
- Unique constraints.
- Check constraints.
- Indexes.
- RLS policies.
- Database dependencies.

Those concerns are audited separately.

No column or object should be removed based solely on this inventory.

## Preliminary Observation

No active/legacy classification is made from column structure alone.

This inventory will later be cross-referenced with:

1. Constraints and foreign keys.
2. Database dependencies.
3. Functions and triggers.
4. RLS policies.
5. Application repository references.
6. Production/historical data considerations.
